# Claim ledger: markets L1 to L3 (concept audit markets-1-3, blind, round 1)

Scope: `app/content/courses/markets/` l1 to l13, review-1 to review-3, homework-1 to homework-3 at commit 6c01af87 (no problems-* file exists for these levels). Stage 1 course, so the full ledger: one row per prose claim, graded key, slip value, wrong option, derivation line, hint, feedback line, explanation, summary idea and model answer. Verdicts: correct, wrong, imprecise, misleading, unverified. `sim:` names a check in `docs/reports/markets-L1-3-concepts.py` (each prints `ok <name>` or `FAIL <name>`). Sources: Hull, *Options, Futures and Other Derivatives* (ch. 1 forwards, ch. 2 futures mechanics, ch. 3 hedging and basis, ch. 4 interest rates, duration and convexity, ch. 5 forward and futures prices, ch. 6 day counts); Fabozzi, *Bond Markets, Analysis and Strategies* (ch. 2 pricing, ch. 3 yields, ch. 4 price volatility); Bodie, Kane and Marcus (BKM), *Investments* (ch. 3 margin and short sales, ch. 5 returns and the Sharpe ratio, ch. 14 and 16 bonds and duration, ch. 18 dividend discount model); Brealey, Myers and Allen, *Principles of Corporate Finance* (ch. 2 and 5, NPV and IRR). A hint or feedback row marked correct was read against the key and the slip value it names; its arithmetic is covered by the check named for its screen.

Rows: 2798. correct 2781, wrong 4, imprecise 12, misleading 1, unverified 0.

| l2-present-value.json | summary.ideas[1] | Discounting is compounding run backwards: divide by $1 + r$ once for each year of waiting. | correct | Fabozzi ch. 2; true for r > 0 (every rate in the lesson is positive) |
| l2-present-value.json | summary.ideas[2] | A stream of cash flows is worth the sum of their present values. A perpetuity is worth $C/r$. | correct | Fabozzi ch. 2; true for r > 0 (every rate in the lesson is positive) |
| l2-present-value.json | summary.formula | Present value: PV = \dfrac{C}{(1 + r)^n} (At $n = 0$ the factor is 1: cash in hand is not discounted.) | correct | Fabozzi ch. 2; true for r > 0 (every rate in the lesson is positive) |
